Holiday Gift Guide 2026

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With the holiday gift-giving season upon us, it’s time to think about the gaming fans in your life and find the ideal present to brighten their day. And if you’re the gamer in the family, we always encourage you to circle relevant items in the coming pages and leave your magazine open in hard-to-miss locations around the house. We’ve scoured dozens of tech products, tabletop games, collectibles, and more to select and highlight some of the coolest items we found this year.

We recommended the Board shortly after its launch, but with its expanded game library and lower price than first-adopter rates, this holiday season is an ideal time to make the leap. This family-friendly platform features original games that sit comfortably between the digital and physical divide. The horizontally placed screen shows the backdrop of one of the several included games, or the sold-separately add-on games that have popped in since launch. Each game has its own real physical play pieces that interact with the action onscreen, often in innovative and charming ways. Think of it as the best of both worlds between board games and video games, and you’ll get what they’re shooting for here. $399

Few additions to the latest Dungeons & Dragons rules pack as much punch and value as this new expansion, focusing on ways to develop further and evolve the use of magic across your tabletop gaming sessions. New subclasses, spells, creatures, magical items, and factions add thoughtful, intriguing options for both players looking to play someone new and DMs hunting for new story hooks and challenges. $50

If you’re as excited as we are about this month’s celebration of the Tomb Raider legacy (see p. 40), this centerpiece statue is an ideal fit for your collection. Standing roughly nine inches in height, Lara is poised for exploration in her original classic garb. The sculpt strikes a perfect balance between crisp detail and stylistic presentation, in line with that early version of the character; the metallic blue motorcycle paint is especially striking. $250

For players longing for the glory days of stand-up arcade games, it’s hard to beat Arcade 1Up’s approach. The team’s sturdy cabinets look and sound great as a marquee feature of your game room. The new Outrun machine features much of the original display art, a bright 19-inch screen, and responsive gas and brake pedals as you steer around those tight corners. Sega has packed in OutRun, Turbo OutRun, OutRunners, and Power Drift. If old-school racing isn’t your thing, look for cabinets that run the gamut from Pac-Man to Joust to Claw Machines. $629.99

The long-running Nendoroid figures continue to be an attractive but still affordable choice for building collections across numerous fan-favorite series. There are somewhere around 2000 characters at this point! New entries like the Genshin Impact collection make a strong case for the line. The look is chibi, the quality is excellent, poseability is high, and customizable face and accessory parts let you change up your display over time. $46

We’ve previously sung the praises of Libernovo’s versatile approach to an ergonomic gaming chair, which includes a lumbar stretch functionality, a steady recline option for comfortable pushback without sudden shifts, and dynamically adjusting parts as you move through your day. The Maxis line does all that, but addresses one of the fundamental problems for at least some users with the original – it wasn’t big enough. With features like a wider base and longer headrest reach, the Maxis aims to provide the same comfort to the players who are tall or wide enough to need the extra breathing room. $1119

Privacy around smart glasses has been a major point of contention as wearable tech continues to expand. Even G2s have a clear answer to that issue by consciously leaving out a camera. Instead, these glasses focus on productivity and specific tasks, like real-time translation into 35 languages, in-lens teleprompters for presentations, and calendar notifications – all presented in a readable display that is largely invisible from the outside. $600

If you’re the player on your team who likes to have easy access to soundboards and real-time voice changing, the Key Pocket could be just the ticket. This small device straps onto your phone for mobile play, but it’s also compatible with consoles, Discord, Twitch, or just about any place you want to tweak audio on the fly. A free tier gives you access to some voice-changing and sounds, while a separate subscription opens up hundreds of thousands of additional options. But be warned: we can’t help you after your friends abandon you for that baby voice you won’t stop using. $99

The question of the best bang for your buck in modern consoles has become extraordinarily complicated, especially as tech prices have soared in recent years. There’s no right answer, but Sony’s high-end machine walks the line of offering extremely high-tier performance without escalating to even higher-priced gaming PCs. Higher and more consistent frame rates than on standard PS5, advanced ray tracing, and an increasing number of games that feature enhanced PSSR image options all add up to a better gaming experience, if you’re ready to make the significant price jump. $900

If the gamer in your life is looking for a stylish, understated way to express their love of the hobby, Anicorn’s designer watch collaboration with PlayStation could be a standout option. Available in black and a slick silver/white combo, the minimalist timepiece includes the familiar face buttons of Sony’s controller encased in an oil-injection display so they slowly and freely float across the watch as you move. $250

It’s a safe bet you’ve at least seen the popular Shashibo puzzle cubes by this point. We’ve been fans of their brain-teasing, fidgety fun for some time. Battle Shapes takes the core concept – reshaping the box into various distinct magnetically-locked shapes – and extends it into a timed game experience, playable solo or head-to-head. By targeting specific shapes, you significantly improve your speed with the toy, and scannable QR codes on the cards offer clear tutorials for each shape so you can prep for competition. $50

While the vast majority of gamers looking for good personal audio are going to be okay with a larger set of cans over their ears, We know more than a few players for whom that’s a definite no – for comfort, awareness of surrounding sounds, or other reasons. The Arc 5 earbuds aim to solve for that, with an over-ear design that feels extremely comfortable. And because they sit on the ear, you can still hear what’s going on around you. While that limits some aspects of the final sound quality, the inclusion of THX certification, Dolby Atmos optimization, and Dynamic Bass Enhancement helps to ensure audio is still very strong. $230

Across Lego’s many recent themes, Tolkien’s legendarium has offered some stellar sets. The largest yet is the capital of Gondor, with an astonishing 8,278 pieces and a height of roughly two feet. The castle/city build is impressive, featuring tons of interesting angles and turns. The finished product aims to offer the best of both worlds in presentation – a microscale representation of the front façade and a reverse minifigure-scale Hall of Kings. We especially like the inclusion of Denethor’s cherry tomatoes. $650

The original Azul is often cited as a foundational entry in modern board gaming. The “Kids” versions of celebrated titles aren’t always a sure bet, but this one holds up to scrutiny. Azul Kids lowers the complexity threshold but keeps the foundational strategic thinking. Players lay out uniquely shaped tiles into mosaic frameworks in colorful, appealing patterns; the basic concept of the original inspiration is clear, and kids as young as five can grasp and enjoy the fun. $25

Many corners of the collecting world have embraced the fun (and frequent despair) of blind boxes, where you never know exactly who you’ll get in a given series. Funism has a strong track record for quality, offering adorable smaller figures like their latest line of Evangelion collectibles. Each figure stands nearly four inches and features both human characters and mecha. You can buy singles and roll the dice, or purchase larger sets with a no-duplicates guarantee, which can save a lot of heartache if you’re trying to line your whole shelf. $21

If you’re going to spring for a high-end keyboard for PC gaming, it’s important that you like the feel of the keys. The slightly smaller overall 75% size profile of this Blackwidow has no impact on the great feel of typing – a clear highlight for the unit. The customizable, colored backlights are also a big selling point, as is the side command dial and built-in OLED display, which can be tweaked to show PC performance, GIFs, lighting brightness, and more. It’s a strong contender in a crowded field, and justifies its price with both comfort and customizability. $300

Beautiful, vibrant art and a refreshing take on the classic area-control board game genre are the two strongest reasons to take a close look at Wroth. Players control one of five races vying for supremacy across a conflict-ridden peninsula. Factions are asymmetric, so everyone has unique capabilities, and that adds significant replayability. Players roll and draft dice, allowing distinct actions that move the game forward. And move it does; unlike many games of this ilk, Wroth can be learned quickly and played to completion in a little over an hour. $65

Console and computer hardware prices are skyrocketing with no sign of slowing, and perhaps as a result, retro gaming handhelds are gaining popularity. Many quality devices are available, but what sets the Evercade Nexus apart is its officially licensed game cartridges. Imagine the nostalgic joy of a Game Boy, but with a cartridge that includes both Banjo-Kazooie and Tooie. The cartridge boxes even include manuals. For fans of physical media, Evercade Nexus is a great option. $199

This tiny USB-C plug-in (with included USB-A adapter) solves a bunch of audio challenges in one uniform device. It offers high-resolution Bluetooth transmission on iPhone, high-bitrate audio transmission to compatible LDAC headphones, enables high-quality Bluetooth output from your PC, and even low-latency Bluetooth audio on PS5 and Switch. In short, it takes a huge number of products with a USB port and ensures a dynamic Bluetooth audio connection with relative ease. $99

The original MV6 is a familiar sight on streams thanks to its clear voice reproduction and overall ease of use. Shure has built on that success with the Gen 2, offering an excellent standalone mic that doesn’t break the bank. Available in black or white, the Gen 2 features a handy auto-level mode that keeps volume even as you adjust your own amplitude, an adjustable denoiser for your space, a longer cable for out-of-the-box convenience, and three presets for plug-and-play use. In short, it’s an excellent choice if you want great performance without needing an audio engineering degree. $169

XR Glasses are still fighting for awareness, but once you try them, it’s easy to see why the concept is on the rise. Viture’s Pro 2 makes an impressive case for adoption, with a competitive price point, comfortable wear, and a giant 146-inch screen that floats in front of your face for gaming or work on your PC or phone. The display is crisp, bright, and impressive, even as the glasses are light and easy to use. With a mobile dock mini (sold separately), you can even snap onto a Switch 2 with ease and start playing instantly. $299

Role-playing gamers have several excellent universal systems to pick between when considering a story or campaign idea that might fall outside traditional genre staples. Among many good options, Cypher stands out as one of the very best. The system uses a rules-light, narrative-focused approach that prioritizes player-driven storytelling across everything from hard sci-fi to superheroes to intrigue- driven espionage. This is the corebook that’s needed to run and play the full system; additional books only add options. $50

As gamers have continually migrated toward personal audio that can rock amazing sound without disturbing the whole house, high-end premium headphones have become a dangerously competitive space. Turtle Beach meets that challenge head-on, with a robust option that sounds beautiful in use. The Stealth Pro II boasts the slick ability to wirelessly switch between different devices, which is awesome if you’re one of those players who regularly jumps between platforms to play your favorite titles. Like the whole category, it’s a lot to spend on a headset, but Dolby Atmos, active noise cancellation, and 60mm dual drivers all add up to a powerful argument. $300

With an increasing number of top-tier console and PC games like Resident Evil Village, No Man’s Sky, and Dead Cells now available on iOS, not to mention the consistent quality of Apple Arcade, Apple phones remain the best mobile platform for gaming. The 18 Pro Max phone has implemented a number of tweaks that strengthen the platform, including higher memory, much faster graphics processing, impressive HDR performance, and notably improved battery life, all adding up to a gaming experience that is working hard to justify your playtime when away from home. Starting at $1299

SteelSeries is best known for its strong audio line-up, but its push into premium-tier controllers exhibits a similar flair for quality and usability. The new Aeon Pro has a lot to recommend it as your go-to Xbox, PC, or mobile choice. The two included batteries (one of which can always be charging in the dock) ensure essentially unlimited playtime. The full-pull or hair triggers and buttons are clicky and satisfying, and the Hall Effect thumbsticks are built to minimize any drift. Textured handgrips and overall heft are ideal. And an onboard OLED lets you adjust profiles and button assignments, switch between platforms, and check battery at a glance. $260

Valve’s pricey VR headset isn’t a revolution in the medium, but the company has been invested in the technology since its inception. As a result, the Steam Frame is a very good, very comfortable headset. Like its competition, the Quest, its best use is playing games locally on the headset, and Steam’s library is vast and interesting. Wireless connected play to a powerful PC is also well done, but currently the included Wireless Adapter is not fully compatible with the Steam Machine – a baffling limitation we were shocked to confirm. VR is an expensive sub-hobby within video games, but Steam Frame is among the best ways to explore and enjoy it. $1059

Many of the coolest “gaming lights” focus on the flexibility and fun of bright RGB customization. While we’re fans of that aesthetic, it leaves others out in the cold who might want something a bit more natural. The Elements hex panels feature a natural wood-inspired panel, customizable “scenes” like a campfire glow, and can react to both music and touch, with adjustable ambient white light to keep the vibe relaxed. $200

Kyle Hilliard contributed to this article.

Cover Reveal – Tomb Raider: Legacy Of Atlantis

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Game Informer’s next cover story is Tomb Raider: Legacy of Atlantis, the upcoming reimagining of the original 1996 Tomb Raider arriving on February 12. Since that game celebrates the franchise’s 30th anniversary this year, we’re doing the same with a double-whammy of Tomb Raider features.

We visited Crystal Dynamics’ office to play Legacy of Atlantis for several hours, including an exclusive tour of the Greece section. For our 12-page cover story, we spoke to key team members at Crystal Dynamics and Flying Wild Hog to learn why it’s tackling Lara Croft’s seminal adventure once again (having already remade the game once in Tomb Raider: Anniversary), how this version of the game differs from previous incarnations and why, discuss the teams' controversial use of AI development tools, and how Legacy of Atlantis plants seeds for the upcoming Tomb Raider: Catalyst.

The issue also includes a six-page retrospective of Tomb Raider’s 30-year history, with insights from Crystal Dynamics. This celebration is reflected in our cover art, designed by Tomb Raider franchise art director Brenoch Adams, which showcases every version of Lara Croft from the Core Design, Legend, Survivor, and current eras.

The December issue also marks the return of our annual (and very large) holiday buyers guide, featuring curated gift suggestions for the gamers in your life ahead of the winter holidays. Furthermore, we’ve created a list of family-friendly games to enjoy with younger relatives during upcoming holiday gatherings. Guest contributor Veerender Singh Jubbal writes about India's emerging game development scene and how its community is helping elevate local titles to an international stage. Ahead of the launch of Ryu Ga Gatoku’s Stranger Than Heaven, executive editor Brian Shea played a new section of the era-spanning Yakuza/Like a Dragon prequel while picking the brains of its lead designers for details on the game’s plot, themes, and time periods. Senior associate editor Wesley LeBlanc shares his glowing impressions of Murals, the colorful, fairy-tale-inspired adventure from Disney Illusion Island developer Dlala Studios, whom he spoke with about the game’s conception.

We also have plenty of previews for upcoming games including Final Fantasy VII Revelation, Warhammer 40,000: Dawn of War IV, Muramasa: Revenant Blades, Fate/Extra Record, Tiny Metal 2, Mr. Records, and Eternal Anima. We also have reviews for Gears of War: E-Day, Castlevania: Belmont’s Curse, Ace Combat 8: Wings of Theve, Control Resonant, Star Wars: Galactic Racer, Silent Hill: Downfall, and many more.

Roehrig: Groves tries to break me mentally - so I can really flourish now

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Lucas Roehrig is being tested to his limits by his trainer George Groves, a former world champion and star of British boxing; London's Roehrig fights Scottish champion Connal McDonald at the Copper Box Arena; Watch Zuffa Boxing 12 on Saturday live on Sky Sports

Sports Journalist @johndennen

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Lucas Roehrig, the protege of elite world champion George Groves, is ready to make his mark.

Roehrig is a richly talented cruiserweight prospect. On Saturday, in his 10th pro fight, live on Sky Sports he takes on Scottish champion Connal McDonald.

For Roehrig this fight is a key step in his career and he wants to make it a breakthrough win.

"I'm looking forward to making a statement," the 23-year-old told Sky Sports. "It's about an A-class performance and really putting on a show."

He is though expecting McDonald to be a threat. "He beat Scott Forrest in his last fight," Roehrig said.

"A good boxer, a good amateur pedigree, a good mover and someone that's going to come to win, come to fight and that's always exciting. He's going to create openings for me. We want a live fight."

Roehrig is the first fighter George Groves, a former WBA world champion and one of the big stars of British boxing, decided to train.

Groves saw something special in the youngster.

"Some stuff which you can't teach is: do you have speed and power - which he has in abundance. He's fun to train, he's fun to be on the journey with, he's going to be an exceptional fighter," Groves told Sky Sports.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Groves was known for his resilience and strong mentality. His work on Roehrig's mind is going to be particularly telling. "There's a lot of sessions where it's physically hard, but the goal is to try and break me mentally," Roehrig said.

Groves explained: "It's now really about your mental preparation. Being on point all the time. I learned that the hard way in some ways. [His fight with James DeGale] was a battle of the mind. I really trained the brain to stay switched on and concentrated for the full duration of the fight but also the full duration of a camp.

"It's something that you do have to work on, you do have to develop and we're doing it."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

He is always subjecting Roehrig to tests, whether the prospect realises it or now. Putting him in to spar with Jai Opetaia, arguably the world's best cruiserweight, before Roehrig had even had a professional fight was one of those tests.

"To see how you can handle the pressure, if you're going to crumble, if you're going to complain," Roehrig recalled. "He pushed me and I didn't fold.

"It's the aura that's around someone like Opetaia, a big puncher, intense even for the sparring.

"I would say that's something I naturally have where I don't panic, I don't ever get too high or too low," he added.

"That's what gives you the confidence on fight night. I've already been through the trenches, I can really flourish now."

Watch Lucas Roehrig vs Connal McDonald this Saturday live on Sky Sports.

KAS is testing the $0.030 to $0.032 demand zone, where buyers need to defend support.

Reclaiming $0.035 to $0.037 and $0.042 to $0.045 could improve Kaspa's recovery structure.

A move toward $0.065 requires several confirmed breakouts; losing $0.030 exposes $0.027 to $0.0285.

The KAS price prediction hinges on one immediate question: can buyers defend the $0.03 to $0.032 demand zone? Kaspa is testing that area after a pullback, and a convincing rebound hasn’t been confirmed yet. Traders looking for a recovery have several resistance levels to watch before any $0.065 target becomes realistic.

The current price is around $0.03186, placing KAS inside its marked local demand range. A clear rejection of lower prices could suggest buyers are returning. But hoping for a bounce and confirming one are two different things.

The next requirement is a higher low. Without that, the recovery remains a chart scenario rather than an established trend reversal.

$KAS: THE BASE HAS TO HOLD FIRSTKAS is testing the $0.030–$0.032 demand area after the latest pullback. A clear rejection of lower prices would provide the first evidence of demand returning. I’m looking for buyers to establish a higher low before expecting a sustained… pic.twitter.com/6LbsfIwoN3

The first hurdle sits at $0.035 to $0.037. A successful reclaim, followed by a pullback that holds, would improve the short-term structure.

Above that, attention shifts to $0.042 to $0.045, identified as key supply. Clearing this zone could bring descending resistance and the recent swing area around $0.050 into focus. Daily confirmation matters; a brief move above resistance isn’t enough.

If KAS clears $0.050 and establishes support, the next major supply zone sits at $0.058 to $0.060. Beyond that, the chart outlines $0.065 as a potential objective.

That’s a substantial move from current levels, but the route isn’t automatic. Each reclaimed resistance area needs to become support before the next advance looks convincing.

The downside is equally clear. Daily acceptance below $0.030 would weaken the KAS price prediction and put deeper support at $0.027 to $0.0285 in focus. For now, the demand reaction matters more than the ambitious target.

Rebecca Gayheart Reveals Shock of Eric Dane’s Death—and How They Made His Hospital Room Feel Like Home in His Final Moments

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Eric Dane's estranged wife, Rebecca Gayheart, has lifted the lid on the shock that came with the actor's death, revealing that she had "no idea that was it."

While speaking to NBC's Maria Shriver on Nov. 3—the same day the late Hollywood star's memoir, "My Book of Days," hit shelves—Gayheart, 55, reflected on the final moments of Dane's life.

The actor, was diagnosed with the degenerative neurological disease in April 2025 and died on the afternoon of Feb. 19, 2026. Gayheart confessed that she and their two daughters, Billie and Georgia, "never, ever anticipated" that he would pass away when he did.

“He had gone to the hospital so many times before, and they would patch him up, so to speak, and he would come home,” Gayheart explained. “And he’d be OK. And we’d go back to the normal routine, which did change week by week.”

She then recalled her estranged husband's first day in the hospital during his first trip there.

“I said, ‘Listen, I’ll be back. I have a dinner. I’m going to this event.’ I had no idea that that was it, that that week was going to be our last week,” Gayheart revealed. “And nor did my daughters. It really took a few days for us to understand the gravity of what that last trip to the hospital meant.”

She noted that she and their daughters began to realize how bad things had become a few days into the hospital visit.

“I think it was like day three in the hospital where it shifted. Everything shifted. Just the tone of when the doctors were talking to us, and some of the information we were given," Gayheart said.

At the same time, Dane was also beginning to come to terms with his last days.

“He was fully aware it was shifting, and he was still communicating with us. Not well verbally, but I could understand him most of the time. The kids could most of the time, his closest friends," she revealed.

Meanwhile, friends of Dane and Gayheart helped her through the emotional turmoil as she struggled with "decision fatigue."

“It was figuring it out as we went, but I remember the moment Eric decided to make some choices, and he did. ... He was in control of the way he was going to leave," she said.

Because they couldn't set up their usual family sleepover at home, they brought everything they needed to the hospital to ensure they could spend Dane's final moments with him.

“The girls and I just camped out there with him, and we had our family sleepovers, and we brought in those little candles that aren’t real candles. They have batteries. And we played his favorite music, and we tried to make it as nice as you can be in the hospital room," she revealed.

Although they cherished their final moments with Dane, Gayheart revealed she wasn't prepared for how quickly the time to say goodbye came.

“A lot of people will say, ‘Well, at least you knew, and you could have those last conversations.' But we didn’t know, because there is no road map. No one can tell you what the journey looks like because it’s so different for every patient. He was becoming more alive mentally, but losing all of his physical abilities. And for the girls, that was really hard to watch as well. But overall, it’s a blur, because it was just so overwhelming," she said.

Dane was meant to meet with Shriver shortly before his death; however, they had to reschedule their plans because the actor wasn't feeling well and mere days later, Dane died.

In Dane's memoir, which was released Nov. 3, he revealed he was initially diagnosed with multifocal motor neuropathy, a treatable autoimmune condition, just months before he received his ALS diagnosis.

Speaking about the book, Gayheart previously told People, she was “grateful he did, because it gives my girls the opportunity to know their dad as a human being.”

In the days leading up to his death, the family found comfort in familiar routines, eating sushi, watching movies, and gathering for game nights.

“We tried to be present through all of our fear and frustration and uncertainty. It was truly an exhausting and heartbreaking experience, but it also showed us his extraordinary strength every day, and that was really wonderful for the girls and myself to witness,” she told People.

In an excerpt previously published by the outlet, Dane recalled the devastating phone call in which he told Gayheart about his diagnosis.

“I say to Rebecca on the phone, ‘It’s what we feared.’ I don’t remember a single word I said after that,” he wrote.

Despite their separation, Dane made clear that he and Gayheart remained a family.

The “Grey’s Anatomy” actor described the news as a “violent impact,” comparing the feeling to “getting the wind knocked out of me.”

As he struggled to process the diagnosis, he recalled hearing Gayheart “wailing on the other end of the phone.”

“My sobs make my body shudder, and I hear her words pouring out amid her tears, ‘I’m here, we’ll fight this together, I’m not going anywhere,’” he wrote.

Throughout the memoir, Dane described how his daughters gave him a sense of purpose as he faced the disease.

“I’m going to show my girls how to walk through this life with grace and dignity, no matter what. I am going to FIGHT. This has become my purpose. My reason. This is now my job,” he wrote.

Dane's death was revealed in a statement by his representatives.

“He spent his final days surrounded by dear friends, his devoted wife, and his two beautiful daughters, Billie and Georgia, who were the center of his world.

"Throughout his journey with ALS, Eric became a passionate advocate for awareness and research, determined to make a difference for others facing the same fight. He will be deeply missed, and lovingly remembered always. Eric adored his fans and is forever grateful for the outpouring of love and support he’s received. The family has asked for privacy as they navigate this impossible time.”

Following his death, many of his celebrity friends have come forward to lay bare the actor's final moments.

Dane and and Gayheart, 54, tied the knot in 2004, and share daughters Billie Beatrice, 15, and Georgia Geraldine, 13.

Gayheart filed for divorce from Dane in 2018. However, in March 2025, just one month before the "Grey's Anatomy" alum announced his diagnosis, she dismissed the filing.

While speaking on the podcast "Broad Ideas," Gayheart had opened up about the state of their relationship, insisting that she will always be there to support Dane, even though their marriage had ended.

“We’ve been separated for eight years. The kids live with me 100% of the time. There’s been lots of just stuff, other stuff,” she told hosts Rachel Bilson and Olivia Allen.

She added that it is important for her to be a "role model" for her daughters, particularly in how she treats her former spouse.

“I am definitely trying to show them that we show up for people, no matter what. He is our family. He is your father. We show up, and we try to do it with some dignity and some grace and just get through it and that we will get through it the best we can," she said.

Gayheart noted that she was staying afloat and helping her loved ones by trying to "stay optimistic."

ALS, also known as Lou Gehrig's disease, is a disorder that affects nerve cells in the brain and spinal cord, according to the ALS Association.

For around 90% of cases of ALS, there is no known family history or a genetic mutation linked to ALS.

For 5% to 10% of cases, there is a family history of the disease. This is often referred to as familial ALS.

There is currently no known cure for ALS. However, there are FDA-approved medications that may help slow down the progression of the disease.

After sharing his diagnosis, Dane closely guarded many of the details of his medical battle.

According to his IMDB page, the actor had three projects that were due to be released: "Euphoria" Season 3, which premiered April 12, 2026; a 13-episode thriller called "Countdown"; and a movie called "Family Secrets."

Dane rose to stardom after appearing in ABC's hit medical drama "Grey's Anatomy," where he played the role of Dr. Mark Sloan, who was often referred to as "McSteamy."

At the time of his death, Dane was believed to be residing in a three-bedroom, three-bathroom pad that spans 2,265 square feet, which he snapped up with Gayheart in 2000 for $949,000.

She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

Allegri: ‘Napoli’s Champions League fate is in our hands, but the league is more important’

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Massimiliano Allegri insists Napoli’s Champions League fate is still in their own hands, but warned that turning their league form around matters more.

With quotes via TuttoMercatoWeb, the Napoli coach spoke at the Estádio do Dragão on Tuesday, before Wednesday’s game against Porto, which kicks off at 21:00 CET [20:00 GMT].

Allegri is back in the dugout after a two-match Serie A ban. During it, Napoli won 4-1 at Monza and lost 2-0 at Juventus, leaving them 10th on 14 points. In Europe, they have three points from three games after defeats in their first two matches and a 4-3 win over Bodø/Glimt.

He opened by paying tribute to Tenerife striker Jesús de Miguel, who died at 30 after a collision with Andorra goalkeeper Owono during a match in Spain. “First of all, I’d like to offer my condolences to the family of Jesús de Miguel for this tragic accident to a young man, before a footballer, who lost his life,” he said.

“Tomorrow’s game is difficult, because Porto have won 10 in a row in the league, and they’ve played some good games in the Champions League too. As for us, talking is of little use, we need to put in a good performance. In the Champions League, our destiny is in our hands, because with 10 points we’d reach the play-offs at least. But then there’s the league to sort out, and that’s the most important thing.”

“Tomorrow isn’t the last chance. With 10 points you go to the play-offs. Tomorrow is an important game. Manchester is an important game but almost impossible. We’ll fight for qualification against Club Brugge, Viking and Sabah,” Allegri said.

He denied the injury crisis takes any pressure off. “It doesn’t take any pressure off, because it’s an important Champions League game against a strong team. Right now there’s no need to talk and explain. There’s only the need to act, to work, to get the injured players back and turn this negative period into a positive one.”

On Rasmus Højlund and Kevin De Bruyne, he said: “Højlund’s fever has gone. De Bruyne will train today, and afterwards we’ll see if he’s available for tomorrow.”

Asked about fears of a repeat of the season Napoli finished 10th, Allegri said: “Right now it’s time to act, not to talk. It’s not an easy moment. We’ve had mixed results and haven’t yet managed to win two league games in a row. But what’s certain is that we have to keep working, get back all the players who will return after the international break, and try to turn things around in the league, where there will be time and space to recover. In the meantime, we’re thinking about tomorrow, there’s a game to play.”

He returned Francesco Farioli’s praise. “I think Farioli, a young coach, has already achieved good results, both in the Netherlands and in Portugal. He has a very important career ahead of him.”

On the threat Porto pose at home: “We know Porto are very aggressive at home, and they have technical, physical and high-quality players. When we have the ball we’ll need to be good technically, and when we don’t we’ll need to defend well, which is something we’ve done less this season.”

Asked whether Porto would press with fewer players or match Napoli’s numbers, he said: “I don’t know, it depends on what Farioli decides. Whether it’s numbers even or an extra man, you have to behave in two completely different ways.”

De-Escalated Treatment for HPV-Related Throat Cancer Gets Long-Term Supporting Data

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

— More than 90% of patients alive without evidence of disease at 5 years

Reduced-intensity treatment for human papillomavirus (HPV)-positive oropharyngeal cancer (OPC) led to durable cancer control in more than 90% of patients, long-term follow-up from a prospective study showed.

After a median follow-up of 5 years, 94% of patients had no evidence of disease and 90.6% were alive at last follow-up after de-escalated elective nodal irradiation (ENI) during definitive concurrent chemoradiation (CRT). Five-year locoregional control, distant metastasis-free survival (DMFS), and overall survival (OS) exceeded 90%. All but one patient with locoregional failure had recurrences in areas with known residual macroscopic disease. No treatment failures occurred in nodal basins that were omitted from treatment.

De-escalation came in the form of 30-Gy ENI instead of the usual 50-60 Gy dose and omission of low-risk nodal basins, reported Nancy Y. Lee, MD, of Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center in New York City, and colleagues in JAMA Oncology.

"These outcomes were attained across the spectrum of T- and N-stage groups and smoking histories among patients predominantly treated with concurrent platinum-based chemoradiosensitization," the authors noted. "The systematic omission of uninvolved nodal levels IB/V did not result in failures in those areas and provides a simple class solution to reducing irradiated volume and toxic effects. The 30-Gy ENI dose remains the lowest among published approaches, is tailored to HPV-associated disease, and results in the lowest reported gastrostomy tube insertion rate."

"Although low-dose ENI without concurrent chemotherapy has been investigated, recent phase III randomized trials have demonstrated the superiority of concurrent platinum-based chemotherapy for achieving cure in HPV-associated OPC, underscoring the importance of de-escalating ENI within an optimally curative paradigm for HPV-associated OPC," they wrote.

The study showed that focusing on radiation dose is not the only strategy to de-escalate treatment for HPV-associated OPC, said David Sher, MD, of UT Southwestern Medical Center in Dallas, who was not involved in the study.

"While we often focus on the total dose delivered to visible cancer, in actuality, ENI is responsible for a substantial amount of normal tissue irradiation and dose to critical normal structures, such as the salivary glands and swallowing structures," Sher, an expert for the American Society for Radiation Oncology, told MedPage Today. "There has been a progressive recognition that ENI dose can be a significant contributor to quality-of-life outcomes, and there are several studies, both prospective and retrospective, that have shown that the standard dose of 50 Gy can be substantially lowered."

"[The] degree of success is remarkable given the extent of dose reduction, and it is exciting to see such superb outcomes," he added. "Although we do not have quality-of-life data from this study, prior reports were also very favorable."

Providing additional context for the study results, Sher noted that all patients underwent PET-CT imaging and the vast majority had MRI as well. Such advanced imaging may play a key role in identifying smaller lymph nodes that might harbor cancer and require higher radiation doses. Additionally, all the patients received cisplatin-containing chemotherapy, and HPV-associated OPC is cisplatin sensitive. Also, the authors did not report how many "equivocal" lymph nodes were treated with a higher radiation dose.

Results of the current study add to those of a previous phase III trial that used a slightly higher nodal irradiation dose, he added.

"All of this work strongly suggests we are delivering ENI doses that are far too high, and we need to be more aggressive in reducing the acute and long-term side-effect burden of our treatments while preserving oncologic efficacy," said Sher. "For such a result to become a true standard of care, though, we do need a phase III randomized study to confirm non-inferiority in a multi-institutional setting, and hopefully such a trial can be available soon to patients and our community."

Lee and colleagues previously reported 2-year outcomes with the de-escalation strategy, showing good locoregional control and survival with "excellent" quality of life. The current report aimed to establish the durability of the strategy, as assessed after a median follow-up of 5 years.

The analysis involved 276 patients treated with intensity-modulated radiotherapy (n=256) or intensity-modulated proton therapy (n=20). Treated nodal and subclinical regions received 30 Gy in 15 fractions and a sequential boost of 40 Gy in 20 fractions.

The 5-year results showed that 260 patients had no evidence of disease at 5 years and 250 were alive at last follow-up. Five-year outcomes were:

A single elective nodal failure occurred 6 months after concurrent CRT in a contralateral indeterminate lymph node present at baseline and treated with 30 Gy, the authors reported. The last regional failure occurred 18 months post-treatment. Distant-only recurrences occurred in 23 patients. At last follow-up, 12 of the patients remained alive with no evidence of disease, and one remained alive with disease.

Mckenna Grace Has A Surprising (And Disney-Oriented) Reason For Saying No To Nude Scenes

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mckenna Grace has been working since she was a little kid, and at 20 years old, it feels like she’s done a bit of everything. She’s done drama, action, horror, romance and more, and in her next project, the book-to-screen adaptation of Sunrise on the Reaping, she’ll play out a deeply intense tragedy that also features massive action. However, while it’s clear she’s open to many kinds of projects and work, there is one thing she says no to: nudity. Surprisingly, her reasoning has to do with Disney.

As I said, when it comes to Grace's career, she's explored lots of different genres, which have put her in many different situations. I mean, in the last year alone, she’s worked on Regretting You, Five Nights at Freddy’s 2, Scream 7, PAW Patrol: The Dino Movie, The Last Kiss, Nimrods and Sunrise on the Reaping. Those are all very different movies, and during an interview with Cosmopolitan, she was asked if there are any kinds of roles she’d say no to. Here’s how she responded, and how nudity and Disney came into the conversation:

At this point, I definitely don’t do nudity or very hypersexual things. I respect anybody who can, but it’s terrifying to me. I don’t know if that’ll ever change. It’s not like I only do wholesome things—my character literally gets gutted by Ghostface in Scream—but my dream is to try and be a Disney princess someday.

See, it’s obvious from her explanation that she has nothing against intimacy or nudity. And it seems like she has a lot of respect for actors who do work on projects that require it or very sexual sequences, like Off Campus, for example. However, it’s not her cup of tea, and I do see why.

As she said, she wants to be a Disney princess someday. If you’ve seen the best Disney movies, you know there’s a certain level of wholesomeness involved. That’s not to say an actor who participates in a project that involves sex scenes or nudity wouldn’t get a role as a Disney princess or prince. However, it’s clear Grace is trying to create an image for herself that makes her an unquestionable fit for one of these very wholesome roles.

I think she’s doing a good job at that too. I mean, before Teagan Croft and Milo Manheim were cast as Rapunzel and Flynn Rider for the live-action Tangled, Mckenna Grace and Mason Thames were popular fan casts for the parts. She legitimately looks like a princess, and her bubbly and bright personality fits right in with that kind of character too.

Now, getting back to what she said, she made it clear she’s not only going to do wholesome roles, and her work on the 2026 movie schedule clearly showcases that. I mean, Scream 7 is a horror movie, and as she said, she gets brutally murdered in it. Also, Sunrise on the Reaping is not for the faint of heart. As a fan of the book, I know how brutal the story is, and I know we’ll see Grace acting out some truly violent and intense moments.

However, while she’s willing to do work like that, she does have boundaries she does not want to cross. That means she’ll say no to projects that are hypersexual or involve nudity, which is totally respectable.

With that said, hopefully someday she’ll get her wish of being a Disney princess. While we wait for that to happen (because I do feel like it’s inevitable), you can catch Mckenna Grace in Sunrise on the Reaping on November 20.

Goalkeeper Jesus Owono in ‘profound distress and anguish’ after death of Tenerife striker Jesus de Miguel

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Andorra goalkeeper Jesus Owono ‘is experiencing profound distress and anguish’ after the death of Tenerife striker Jesus de Miguel.

De Miguel suffered head and chest injuries following a collision with Owono, who was sent off for his challenge near the halfway line, during the closing stages of the Spanish second-division match on Monday.

Owono had sprinted from his goal to clear a long ball when his knee collided with De Miguel’s chest.

De Miguel rolled on the floor immediately after impact but was then motionless on his back as several players urgently signalled for medical help.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

The 30-year-old’s condition was first described as ‘stable, given the seriousness of the condition’ on Monday night but Tenerife issued a second update in the early hours of Tuesday morning which said that his ‘medical condition has deteriorated neurologically.’

Later on Tuesday morning, Tenerife announced that De Miguel had died in hospital.

Owono initially went to leave the pitch after his red card but then turned back after the severity of De Miguel’s situation became clear.

The 25-year-old, who was playing for La Liga side Alaves last year and has made 47 appearances for Equatorial Guinea’s national team, deleted his Instagram account after receiving abuse.

According to Spanish radio station COPE, some supporters at the Heliodoro Rodriguez Lopez stadium shouted ‘murderer’ at Owono, while the goalkeeper is said to have been crying and praying in the dressing room after the match was abandoned.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

Meanwhile, Venancio Tomas Ndong Micha, the president of the Equatorial Guinean Football Federation, has issued his ‘full solidarity and support’ to Owono.

‘It is with deep shock and immense sorrow that I have received the news of the tragic passing of your player, Jesus de Miguel. On behalf of the Equatorial Guinean Football Federation, and on my own behalf, I wish to express our most sincere and heartfelt condolences for this irreparable loss,’ Ndong Micha said in a statement.

‘Football is a family that, in moments like this, unites in grief. We wish to extend our deepest solidarity to his family, his teammates, the coaching staff, and all the blanquiazul (blue-and-white) fans, who are currently enduring a time of indescribable sadness.

‘Furthermore, I feel it is necessary to convey our full solidarity and support at this time to our player, Jesus Lazaro Owono.

‘We know that, as an involuntary participant in this fatal incident during the match, he is experiencing this situation with profound distress and anguish.

‘The Equatorial Guinean Football Federation offers him our full personal and professional support, fully aware of the severity of this event—something no one in our sport ever wishes to witness.

‘We share in your mourning and remain entirely at your disposal. Please accept our deepest respect and support during these most difficult times.’

On Tuesday, police in Spain opened an investigation for possible involuntary manslaughter following De Miguel’s death.

Judicial authorities in the Canary Islands said it is a routine procedure as any death deemed non-natural under Spanish law automatically triggers a judicial inquiry.

However, the chief prosecutor of Santa Cruz de Tenerife, Jose Luis Sanchez-Jauregui, has said it would be ‘very difficult’ for a criminal process to be opened as De Miguel’s collision with Owono occurred during the match.

Speaking to Spanish news agency EFE, Sanchez-Jauregui explained that long-standing Spanish Supreme Court precedent distinguishes injuries sustained during a match from deliberate assaults which are committed when play is halted.

Sanchez-Jauregui added that the referee’s report and the decision of the Sports Disciplinary Committee, which will determine whether Owono intended to cause harm with his challenge on De Miguel, will be considered before any legal action.

On Tuesday afternoon, the Royal Spanish Football Federation (RFEF) announced that all of its football competitions have been suspended until November 6.

Death toll rises in Ukraine after Russia attacks aid centre, hospice and hospital

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Politics  |  Read Source →

Last month, the UN said last month that civilian casualties in Ukraine were at a "record level"

Russian strikes on Ukraine â including on an aid centre, a hospice and a hospital â have killed at least 38 civilians and injured scores of others across Ukraine over the last 24 hours.

At least 33 people are known to have died in a glide bomb attack in the southern Zaporizhzhia region on Monday night, and President Volodymyr Zelensky said the number of victims was set to rise as more than 50 people could be trapped under the rubble.

He called the strike in Komyshuvakha village "one of the largest mass killings committed by Russia during this war".

Ukraine had proposed to extend and expand last week's ceasefire in the Kherson region which allowed people from the frontline Russian-occupied town of Oleshky to evacuate, Zelensky added.

The attack on a school building being used as an aid distribution centre in Komyshuvakha was Moscow's response, he said.

Elsewhere, a hospice was attacked in the central city of Dnipro and a hospital was hit in the north-eastern city of Kharkiv, where Zelensky said 73 people were wounded, most of them elderly patients.

Nine 3,000kg aerial bombs hit Komyshuvakha as residents were gathering to receive humanitarian aid at a distribution centre, authorities said. Local media reported that when the attack began, people took refuge underground but the roof collapsed, trapping many underneath.

"The scale of the tragedy is difficult to comprehend," regional head Mykhailo Semikin said.

Russia's defense ministry said the attack had targeted Ukrainian military units and killed 25 servicemen.

However, there was no credible evidence that the site was being used by the Ukrainian military and there were no major military or industrial facilities in the settlement of Komyshuvakha, reports BBC News Russian defence correspondent Ilya Abishev.

The village is to the south-east of the city of Zaporizhzhia, which has come under constant Russian bombardment.

According to Abishev, Komyshuvakha is of high military importance as part of Ukraine's defensive and transport network on the approaches to the regional capital.

But the UN's human rights monitoring mission said the distribution of humanitarian aid at the local school had been highly publicised and the attack "seems to have violated the most fundamental principles of international humanitarian law", external.

Further north, in Dnipro, nearly 50 terminally ill patients were being transferred to other facilities after their hospice was destroyed by a Russian missile on Monday night. The oldest of the patients was 90 years old.

Mayor Boris Filatov posted photos on Telegram showing elderly people lying in their beds in a hospice room with blown-out windows and rubble and broken glass strewn on the floors.

Dozens of terminally ill patients had to evacuate their hospice after it was hit

"I realise that I am violating ethics," wrote Filatov. "I want as many people as possible to see these photos. Especially politicians and diplomats. Especially abroad.

"These are elderly, terminally ill people. This is a Russian missile."

Elsewhere in Dnipro, a separate strike killed two people and left 18 others wounded. Another five people were killed in aerial bomb and drone strikes on Ukraine's eastern Donetsk region.

Russia has also struck energy infrastructure in nine regions, affecting water and heating.

The water supply in Kyiv was entirely cut off "due to enemy attacks and damage to the capital's energy infrastructure", the main water supplier Kyivvodokanal said. The entire metro network was suspended on Tuesday and Mayor Vitali Klitschko said the situation surrounding the city's power supply was becoming "very difficult".

As Ukraine was counting its dead, the Kremlin announced it would take part in three-way peace talks with Ukraine brokered by the US in Abu Dhabi on 10-11 November.

It will be the first trilateral meeting since February. Since then, Russia and the US have begun engaging more directly, with US President Donald Trump announcing a deal to import Russian diesel.

Speaking to reporters in Berlin on Tuesday, Zelensky said that it was important for Russia to engage seriously in negotiations on a ceasefire and a peaceful settlement, but that he had so far had not seen "any indication" of that being the case.

Several rounds of ceasefire talks late last year yielded no results, and in public declarations Russian officials have generally refused to budge from Moscow's maximalist demand of effective Ukrainian surrender.

US First Lady Melania Trump Melania Trump, who was credited with brokering a truce last week in the besieged town of Oleshky, is set to host representatives of Russia and Ukraine at the White House on Thursday. However, no details were given in the White House statement of who was due to attend the meeting., external

The UN said last month that civilian casualties in Ukraine had reached a "record level", as Russian armed forces resumed systematic attacks on energy infrastructure.

Russia launched a full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2022. Its advances on the front line have since stalled, and attacks on Ukraine have intensified, with Moscow employing large numbers of missiles and drones to strike Ukrainian cities.

At the same time, Ukraine is faces a critical air defense shortage due to stalled Western deliveries of weapons and a lack of American Patriot interceptor missiles.

In Berlin, Zelensky acknowledged that although the front line was stable "the situation in the air was not as positive, particularly when it comes to our air defence capabilities and the protection of our civilian population".

Everyone thinks Brighton and Brentford are bulletproof - Premier League history says otherwise

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation plus sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Play the viral 442GOALS game and build an XI of Premier League legends!

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Fantastic football content straight to your inbox! From the latest transfer news, quizzes, videos, features and interviews with the biggest names in the game, plus lots more.

Sign up to our FREE live football newsletter, tracking all of the biggest games available to watch on the device of your choice. Never miss a kick-off!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The Seagulls and the Bees must avoid certain pitfalls to stay ahead

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Spare a thought, as Brighton and Brentford meet on Sunday, for the rest of the Premier League, forced to watch on as the footballing world swoons over those pesky poster boys.

Just look at them, with their fancy algorithms and flawless recruitment, making it all look so easy. Can’t they mess up just one managerial appointment? Must they hoover up all the midfield talent from Mali?

It is hard not to be impressed by the pair’s accomplishments. Brighton and Brentford were both in League One when their current owners took control. Under Tony Bloom and Matthew Benham, boyhood fans turned professional gamblers, Brighton and Brentford have risen to the upper echelons of the Premier League, earning admirers aplenty along the way. In this season of flux, they could challenge for the Champions League places - or perhaps even more.

Brighton and Brentford have become part of the top-flight furniture. It is easy to assume they will continue bloodying noses and showing up richer rivals for years to come.

Yet the high-flying duo would not have to look too hard for success stories gone wrong: the Championship and League One are littered with clubs once hailed as Premier League models whose fortunes subsequently unravelled.

Wimbledon consistently punched above their weight in the 1990s, finishing in the top nine in three out of four seasons without even having a stadium to call their own. Bolton Wanderers enjoyed two seasons in Europe in the 2000s, and came within three points of qualifying for the Champions League in the middle of that decade.

The 2010s brought the rise of Southampton and Swansea City, who were mixing it with the big boys not long after promotion. Then there was Leicester City, champions in 2015/16, FA Cup winners in 2020/21, and European semi-finalists in 2021/22.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

None of those teams are currently in the Premier League. Leicester and Wimbledon are languishing in League One, where Bolton recently spent five consecutive campaigns, while Swansea have been stuck in the Championship for eight years. Charlton Athletic, another side once heralded as a shining light for middle powers, have not had a seat at the top table for almost two decades.

It is tempting to assume Brighton and Brentford are run too well to suffer a similar fate. Certain factors do suggest they have more staying power than the beacons of old. But declines that appear impossible beforehand can come to seem inevitable afterwards. Brighton and Brentford should not take anything for granted.

They have already avoided one common pitfall. Bolton and Charlton’s achievements were tied to a single manager: Sam Allardyce and Alan Curbishley, respectively. When they left, the operation fell apart. Charlton were relegated the season after Curbishley’s resignation. Bolton survived for longer, but they failed to finish in the top half post-Allardyce.

Swansea and Southampton did a better job of sustaining success across managerial regimes. Swansea’s ascent began under Brendan Rodgers, before they won a trophy under Michael Laudrup and finished eighth with Garry Monk in the dugout. Southampton secured top-eight finishes under Mauricio Pochettino, Ronald Koeman and Claude Puel.

However, one bad appointment can start a downward turn. Puel never fully convinced at Southampton and his successor, Mauricio Pellegrino, really struggled. Swansea became too short-termist, with none of Francesco Guidolin, Bob Bradley, Paul Clement or Carlos Carvajal lasting long.

Wimbledon’s decline had several causes, chief among them the absence of a permanent home. But while the appointment was defensible at the time, Egil Olsen could not maintain the standards set by Joe Kinnear. The former Norway boss favoured the sort of direct football for which Wimbledon were famous, but the dressing room did not take to a former communist who walked to training and wore wellies on the touchline. Olsen was too eccentric even for the Crazy Gang.

The Wimbledon example highlights another potential hazard. It is important to have an identity, but adhering too rigidly to it is risky. Given they were in the top flight for 14 years, Wimbledon could have varied their style earlier. Yet there is a different lesson to learn from Swansea and Southampton, who lost their way after moving too far away from the principles that initially brought them success.

Continually making smart decisions in the transfer market is not easy. Allardyce’s Bolton paired overseas stars (Jay-Jay Okocha, Nicolas Anelka) with experienced British professionals (Gary Speed, Kevin Davies) to good effect, but that model left with the manager. Southampton (Virgil van Dijk, Sadio Mane, Dusan Tadic) and Swansea (Michu, Wilfried Bony, Gylfi Sigurdsson) unearthed more gems than most, but eventually the well ran dry.

As for Leicester, they were always going to struggle to find another Riyad Mahrez or N’Golo Kante lurking in the French second tier. In the end, they were undone by the belief that they belonged among the league’s leading lights. Leicester allowed their wage bill to run out of control, setting the wheels in motion for a demise that brought back-to-back relegations.

Brighton and Brentford have already avoided many of those mistakes. Brighton have thrived under Graham Potter, Roberto De Zerbi and now Fabian Hurzeler. They have evolved their style at the right times, shifting from the pragmatism of Chris Hughton to the possession play of Potter and De Zerbi, to which Hurzeler has added a higher press this term.

Thomas Frank’s exit could have been fatal for Brentford, but they instead moved seamlessly to Keith Andrews. They remain a physical team who like to get the ball forward quickly, but Andrews has added his own ideas to the mix without ripping up the formula that got the Bees here in the first place.

There are no indications that Brighton and Brentford’s data-led recruitment models are about to fail them, although it is possible that other clubs will eventually close the gap. And while it has not happened so far, Brighton and Brentford are not immune from making a poor managerial pick. Only a few months ago, many Brighton fans wanted Hurzeler out, fearing they were destined for decline with the German at the helm.

The manager is often called the most important person at a football club, but that is not the case at Brighton or Brentford. As long as Bloom and Benham remain in place, supporters of both sides can be confident of their continued progress. Leicester’s fall from grace had much to do with the tragic death of owner Vichai Srivaddhanaprabha. Gao Jisheng did not invest in Southampton the way their fans hoped, while the takeover of an American consortium at Swansea was contested from the start.

Virtually every club in England can learn something from Brighton and Brentford, who are more than the sum of their parts at every level. But football has a habit of turning today’s trailblazers into tomorrow's cautionary tales. For Brighton and Brentford, the challenge now is to make being a model club more than a passing phase.

Greg Lea is a freelance football journalist who's filled in wherever FourFourTwo needs him since 2014. He became a Crystal Palace fan after watching a 1-0 loss to Port Vale in 1998, and once got on the scoresheet in a primary school game against Wilfried Zaha's Whitehorse Manor (an own goal in an 8-0 defeat).
